Let's analyze random performance more rigorously. For a_i independent with mean μ, variance σ^2, S ≈ n μ, b_k = sum_{i+j=k} a_i a_j. E[b_k] ≈ μ^2 m_k where m_k = min(k+1,n,2n-1-k). Var(b_k) involves covariances. For independent, products share variables if pairs overlap, some covariance. For uniform [0,1], μ=0.5, σ^2=1/12. Product mean 0.25. Var(product) = E[a^4 b^4?]. For independent X,Y uniform: E[X^2 Y^2]=1/9, mean^2=1/16, var=5/144≈0.0347. Sum of m terms covariance? Non-overlap zero; overlap for terms involving same variable. For b_k, terms (i,k-i). Overlap when i equal or i = k-i'? Pairs share a variable if indices sets {i,k-i} intersect. This can happen when i = j (same pair), i = k-j' => etc. Cov terms O(k). Var ~ m Var(product) + O(k^2)? The O(k^2) due shared variables may dominate? Let's compute. For k central, terms t_i = X_i X_{k-i}. Cov(t_i,t_j) nonzero if {i,k-i} intersects {j,k-j}. For central, many? If i=j same. If i = k-j, then pair {i,k-i} = {k-j,j}, same pair reversed? Actually term j = (k-j)*j, if j=k-i, it's same product? But summation over i from max to min counts each unordered? In convolution b_k = sum_i X_i X_{k-i}. If k even, i=k/2 term X_i^2 counted once. For i and k-i both in sum separately? Yes convolution sums over all i, so if 0<=k-i<n, includes both i and k-i as distinct terms, except when i=k-i. So t_i and t_{k-i} = X_{k-i} X_i = same product, perfect correlation. Thus there are double counting. That doubles mean? Wait m_k number of valid i. central ~n. For k=n-1? If reversed? P(x)^2 coefficient sum i X_i X_{k-i}. Since convolution not autocorr, terms are ordered pairs; t_i and t_{k-i} same product. So b_k has pairs double except middle. But expectation μ^2 m_k still. Variance has covariances for i and k-i exactly same, causing var ~2? Actually t_i=t_{k-i}. If central with n terms, can group m/2 pairs double. So variance similar to sum of double products. Could be O(k^2)? Let's compute for common indices: Let b_k=∑_i X_i X_{k-i}. For independent X. Write as ∑_i X_i X_{k-i}. Cov with itself = E[b_k^2]-mean^2. Expand ∑_{i,j} E[X_i X_{k-i} X_j X_{k-j}]. Nonzero when indices match. Cases: j=i gives E[X_i^2]E[X_{k-i}^2] unless i=k-i. j=k-i gives E[X_i X_{k-i}^2] etc. Many. But order O(m) (only pairs with two equal indices) because if sets {i,k-i} and {j,k-j} overlap. Overlap possibilities: i=j; i=k-j (then sets same); k-i=j (same); k-i=k-j (j=i). So only i or k-i. Thus O(m) terms, not O(m^2). So variance O(m). Good.

Subgradient/Lp update:
Given a (sum n), compute b. We want reduce b's largest coefficients. For L_p with p maybe 4 or 8, gradient G_i = 2p ∑ b_k^{p-1} a_{k-i}. This tends to reduce products contributing to large b. Multiplicative update a_i *= exp(-η G_i / scale). Need preserve sum. However if G_i correlated with a_{mirror}, it can make sequence monotone. We can tune p, η. Could get trapped but multi-start.

Since random independent centered around some mean shape, we could optimize the mean shape f on continuous grid using this gradient with small n_cg (e.g. 512) first, then interpolate to n and optimize. But maybe direct n=4096 enough.

Another algorithm: "alternating minimization" of max coefficient: Represent q_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. If we fix weights a, to reduce a coefficient b_k, we could reduce a at positions i where a_{k-i} large. This is like edge removing in a complete graph with edge weights a_i a_j; we choose vertex weights to minimize max sum of edges per diagonal. This is a continuous quadratic energy minimization. Could use greedy: For current top diagonal k*, perform update a_i = a_i / (1 + ε a_{k*-i}) for all i? Because b_k = a inner reverse(a shifted). Dividing by 1+... reduces inner product. Then normalize. Repeat for top diagonals. This is simple and may reduce peaks without FFT gradient.

For a target diagonal k, to reduce b_k, update a_i *= (1 + γ a_{k-i})^{-1} (valid for k-i). This reduces b_k roughly. Then normalize sum to n. Apply to top T diagonals or sequential. Could be effective.

Maybe use "ratio" update: For all k with b_k > τ, a_i *= exp(-η ∑ b_k a_{k-i}). This is gradient.

Let's compute complexity. n=8192/16384. FFT each update. If we do 1000 updates, each FFT pair ~0.05-0.2s => 50-200s. Good. We can evaluate score (max of b) from same FFT; no extra for trials if we use fixed step or few line searches.

Need ensure nonnegative and bounded. Use log-space:
loga = log(a) + maybe small floor. Update loga -= η * G_norm. Then a=exp(loga); normalize sum=n. But if loga very negative, a underflows; set floor? Since zeros allowed but full support maybe good. Use max(loga, log(1e-12*n?)). For robust, after exp, clip to [1e-12,1000], then normalize.

Step size: G_i scale? b~n, b^{p-1} huge. Normalize G by n^{p-1}? Let's set p=3 or 4. For p=4, b_k^3 ~ n^3, a ~1, G~n^3? We can scale G by max(G) or by n^{p-1}. Step η small, e.g. 0.01 in log. Use line search over η values [1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,0.1] choosing best score. But after update, need compute b again for each trial; costly. Could use small fixed schedule and occasional FFT eval. Or use approximate score from b? Need exact.

Alternative use additive gradient with projection: a_new = np.clip(a - η*G/scale,0); normalize. This can be fast. But multiplicative easier for nonnegative.

Can we derive a Newton/softmax update for minimax? At each step, identify active set A where b_k ≥ max(0, threshold = max_b*(1-δ)). Compute G_i = 2∑_{k∈A} a_{k-i}. Then do `a *= exp(-η G)/(normalize)`. If threshold too high, only very peaks; if low, smooth. Could use p weight instead of 0/1.

We can also exploit polynomial: Computing G via FFT. Let's define function `grad_from_weights(a, w_k, n)`:
Input a length n, w length L=2n-1 (nonnegative). Need G_i = ∑_{k} w_k a_{k-i}, with 0≤k-i<n. We can compute cross-correlation as described.
Let N = power of 2 >= 2n-1 + n? Actually to compute linear correlation without circular alias, need fft length M >= (len(a)+len(w)-1) = n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2? Wait convolution of a (n) and reverse(w) (2n-1) length 3n-1. But we only need correlation where output indices 0..n-1; circular alias if M < n+(L)-1 =3n-2. If using FFT of a and w directly with length M >= 3n-1 to avoid alias. But maybe we can compute derivative with a different zero padding length 2n-1? Let's check.
We want G_i = ∑_{m=0}^{n-1} w_{i+m} a_m. This is cross-correlation of w (length 2n-1) and a (length n) for lags i=0..n-1 (w shifted relative to a). The full linear correlation length n+(2n-1)-1=3n-1. Core functions top-level:
- `next_pow2(x)`
- `convolve_fft(a, N=None)`: returns b length 2n-1 real. Use `N = next_pow2(2*n-1)`. `f = np.fft.rfft(a, N)`, `b = np.fft.irfft(f*f, N)[:2*n-1]`. Clip tiny negative to 0.

- `gradient_weights(a, b, p, scale)`: w = (b/scale)^(p-1). Then G via corona/convolution as above.

Optimization loop:
For each initial candidate:
  a = normalize(candidate,n)
  loga = log(a)
  compute b,max.
  best_local = a,b
  for iter in range(max_iters) until time:
    b = conv(a)
    maxb = max(b)
    score = 2*maxb/n
    if score < best: update
    if maxb <= n/2 * (1+tol) maybe stop? lower bound n/(2n-1)? ideal n/2? Actually min max >= sum_b/(2n-1)=n^2/(2n-1)=n/2 * 2n/(2n-1) ≈ n/2. Score lower bound ≈1. Use threshold 1.001 impossible maybe. Stop if improvement small.
    weights = active or p.
    G = grad(a,weights)
    scale = max(G) + eps
    for step in steps:
       a_t = apply(loga - step*G/scale); normalize; b_t=conv; score_t
       if score_t < best_score: accept; loga=log(a_t); break
    if no accept, maybe try random perturbation or reduce step; if stuck, break to next initial.

But this optimizes only using current b. Since p=3/4 smooth. Need maybe p high to target max; but high p weights peak sharply. Could use adaptive: choose weights = (b / maxb)^{q} with q maybe 4. That emphasizes top peaks. Or active threshold: `w = np.where(b > alpha*maxb, (b/maxb)**q, 0)`.

Let's consider update direction. If b_k peak, G_i = a_{k-i}. Multiplying a_i by exp(-η a_{k-i}) reduces all pairs for that k. This tends to lower the factor at positions paired with high values for peak k. For peak at s≈1.2 (right of center), G_i ∝ a_{k-i}; for k>n, k-i ranges. It may lower certain positions. Repeating may shape density.

Maybe need also allow increasing some a_i to rebuild sum after reducing peaks; normalization increases all uniformly, but relative changes shape. Good.

Potential failure: Multiplicative update can drive distribution to two-point extremes, score high. Need regularize: add small entropy / smoothing. For example, after update, blend with a smooth prior (e.g. previous local or uniform) to avoid collapse: `a = (1-β)*a_t + β*prior`, or in log: `loga = γ loga_trial + (1-γ) log_prior`. Prior could be initial shape or uniform. This acts as regularization. Use small β=0.01.

Alternatively use additive step with projection and L2 regularization: minimize modified objective + ε∑ (a_i - prior_i)^2. Gradient add 2ε(a-prior). But not necessary.

Local optimizer details:
Pseudo:
```
def local_optimize(a, rng, time_limit, verbose):
    n = len(a)
    a = a.astype(float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: uniform; a/=s
    loga = np.log(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a)
    prior_log = loga.copy()
    step_base=0.05
    p=3.0
    last_improve=time
    iter=0
    while time < time_limit and (time-last_improve)<stall_limit:
       b=conv(a); maxb=b.max(); score=2n*maxb
       if score < best_score: best...
       if score <= 1.0001: break
       # maybe every 20 iters try random perturbation? 
       avg = 1.0/(2*n-1)
       # weights: maybe adaptive p: if score>1.8 p=3 else p=4? use p=4
       ratio = np.maximum(b,0)/avg
       if score > 1.8: p_use=2 else p_use=4? 
       w = np.power(ratio, p_use-1)
       # optional top threshold: keep top 20%
       if score >1.6: tau = maxb*0.7 else tau=maxb*0.85
       w = np.where(b >= tau, w, 0)
       G = gradient(a,w,n)
       G = np.maximum(G,0)
       gscale = np.mean(G)
       if gscale <=1e-15: break
       Gn = G/gscale
       trial_best=None; trial_score=best_score
       for step in (step_base*0.5, step_base, step_base*2):
          log_t = loga - step*Gn
          # mix prior
          if mix>0: log_t = (1-mix)*log_t + mix*prior_log
          log_norm, a_t = normalize_log(log_t)
          score_t=score_fft(a_t)
          if score_t < trial_score - 1e-12:
             trial_best=a_t; trial_score=score_t; best_step=step; best_log=log_norm
       if trial_best is None:
          # Try random block perturbation as escape
          a_t = random_perturb(best_a, rng)
          score_t=score_fft(a_t)
          if score_t < best_score: accept; update log
          else:
             step_base *= 0.6; if step_base<1e-4 break
       else:
          accept; step_base = min(2.0, best_step*1.2 if improved else step_base*0.8)
          # update prior_log slowly: prior_log = 0.9*prior_log+0.1*loga? maybe
```
Need ensure `score_fft` not too expensive: It computes b then score. In loop we already compute b for G; can reuse b for score. For trial, compute score_fft which computes b. Could also compute b for G and b score separately. For trials, okay.

`gradient_from_weights`:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1; N=1; while N<L: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    w_rev=np.zeros(N); w_rev[:L]=w[::-1]
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w_rev,N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    idx = np.arange(n)
    G = conv[L-1 - idx]
    return np.maximum(G,0)
```
Potential off-by-one due `np.fft.irfft` length N; okay.

`convolve_fft`:
```
def convolve_fft(a):
 n=a.size; N=1; while N<2*n-1: N*=2
 fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]; return np.maximum(b,0)
```

`normalize_log` implementation:
```
def normalize_log(loga):
    m = float(np.max(loga))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        n=loga.size
        loga = np.full(n, -np.log(n))
        return loga, (1.0/n)*np.ones(n)
    z = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        n=loga.size
        loga = np.full(n, -np.log(n))
        return loga, (1.0/n)*np.ones(n)
    log_norm = loga - (m + np.log(s))
    a = np.exp(log_norm)
    # if a has zeros due underflow, sum maybe <1; renormalize
    asum = float(a.sum())
    if asum > 0 and np.isfinite(asum):
       a = a / asum
       # recompute log to avoid drift? Could use log_a = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
       # But log_norm maybe underflow zeros; if we keep log_norm with -inf, next update bad. Use log of a for next.
    loga2 = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    return loga2, a
```
If we recompute log from a, underflow zeros become -690, okay. But if many values underflow, dynamics lost. Step sizes prevent extreme. Use tiny=1e-300; log=-690. The max log maybe 0. Good.

But recomputing log every iteration from `a` may be expensive but okay. Or keep log_norm and replace nonfinite with -690. Since sum normalized exactly, log of a may be slightly off if a sum not 1; use a/a.sum and log. Simpler.

Should we compute G using top weights only? Let's implement adaptive weights:
```
def make_weights(b, n, score, rng):
   avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
   ratio=np.maximum(b,0)/avg
   # If score high, use p=2 (smoother) to reduce broad peak; if score lower, p=4 to shave max.
   if score > 1.8: p=2.0
   elif score > 1.55: p=3.0
   else: p=4.0
   w = np.power(ratio, p-1)
   # top threshold: keep values with b >= 0.6*maxb maybe
   tau = 0.6*maxb if score>1.8 else 0.8*maxb
   w = np.where(b >= tau, w, 0.0)
   # Normalize weights sum to 1? Not needed; G scale by mean. But weights sum affects G. Use w/=n maybe? 
   return w
```
If score near lower bound, using p=4 may cause too much focusing; line search handles.

Let's design `local_search_top` carefully.

```
def local_search_top(a, rng, time_limit, label, verbose=True):
    n=a.size
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a=a/s else a uniform
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a)
    base_step=0.03
    T = max(64, min(1024, n//16))? n=24576->1024. Maybe 512.
    stall_iters=0
    last_score=best_score
    iter=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    end=start+time_limit
    while time.monotonic() < end:
       b=convolve_fft(a)
       maxb=float(b.max())
       score=2.0*n*maxb/(a.sum()**2) # a sum1
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; stall_iters=0
       else:
           stall_iters +=1
       if score <= 1.00005: break
       if stall_iters>50: # try random reset from best
           a = apply_random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.05)
           loga=np.log(max(a, tiny)); base_step=max(0.01,base_step*0.5); stall_iters=0; continue
       if verbose and iter%100==0: print(label, ...)
       avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
       # T adaptive: if score high, T smaller? use 512
       t = T
       part = np.argpartition(b, -t)[-t:]
       rk = np.maximum(b[part], avg) # avoid zero
       ratio = rk / avg
       if score > 1.9: p_use=1.5? weights linear? p-1 exponent. For p=2 exponent1. If score high, broad; p_use=2.0.
       else if score >1.6: p_use=3.0 else p_use=5.0
       w=np.power(ratio, p_use-1)
       wsum=w.sum(); if wsum<=0: break; w/=wsum
       G=np.zeros(n,float)
       # loop
       for jj in range(t):
           k=int(part[jj]); wi=float(w[jj]); if wi==0: continue
           i0 = k-(n-1)
           if i0<0: i0=0
           i1 = k
           if i1>=n: i1=n-1
           # valid
           if i1 < i0: continue
           stop = k - i1 - 1
           # if stop >=0? use slice
           G[i0:i1+1] += wi * a[k-i0 : stop : -1]
       gmean = G.mean()
       if gmean <= 1e-18:
           # maybe use full FFT gradient or break
           break
       Gn = G / gmean
       # Ensure no NaN
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(Gn)): break
       # line search
       steps = [base_step*0.5, base_step, base_step*2.0]
       best_trial=None
       for st in steps:
           # optionally mix uniform
           log_t = loga - st*Gn
           # add tiny uniform prior? loga_min?
           loga_t,a_t=normalize_log(log_t)
           sc=score_fft(a_t)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
               best_trial=(st,loga_t,a_t,sc)
       if best_trial is None:
           # try larger random or smaller step? one random block
           a_t = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.03)
           sc_t=score_fft(a_t)
           if sc_t < best_score - 1e-12:
               a=a_t; loga=np.log(max(a,tiny)); best...; base_step=abs(base_step)
           else:
               base_step *= 0.6
               if base_step < 1e-5: break
       else:
           st,loga_t,a_t,sc_t=best_trial
           a=a_t; loga=loga_t; best_a=a.copy(); best_score=sc_t; base_step=min(0.2, st)
           stall_iters=0
       iter+=1
    # final maybe compute exact? outside
    return best_a, best_score
```

def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
   n=a.size
   c=a.copy()
   # global multiplicative
   c *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n))
   # optionally block
   width=max(1,n//20); start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width)); c[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
   c=np.maximum(c,0); s=c.sum(); if s>0: c/=s
   return c
```

Random block function:
```
def block_perturb(a,rng,sigma_range,width_range):
   c=a.copy(); n=a.size
   width=rng.integers(*width_range); start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
   c[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma))
   if rng.random()<0.2: c *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma*0.5,size=n))
   c=np.maximum(c,0); s=c.sum(); if s>0 c/=s
   return c
```

```
def add_candidate(pool, score, arr, cap):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    arr = np.asarray(arr,float).copy(); ...
    if len(pool)<cap: pool.append((score,arr))
    else:
       max_idx=0
       for i in range(1,len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] > pool[max_idx][0]: max_idx=i
       if score < pool[max_idx][0]: pool[max_idx]=(score,arr)
```

Detailed `local_search` code:
```
def local_search(a, rng, time_limit, label, target_stop=1.45):
    n = a.size
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12:
       a = np.ones(n)/n
    else:
       a /= s
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fft(a)
    base_step = 0.05
    T = int(min(512, max(64, n // 32)))
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    end_time = start_time + time_limit
    stall = 0
    iter_count = 0
    last_print = start_time
    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
       b = convolve_fft(a)
       maxb = float(b.max())
       sum_a = float(a.sum())
       score = 2.0*n*maxb/(sum_a*sum_a)
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score; stall=0
       else:
          stall +=1
       if best_score <= target_stop:
          break
       if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20:
          print(f"[{label}] iter={iter_count} current={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} step={base_step:.4f}")
          last_print=time.monotonic()
       if stall > 80:
           # random restart from best
           a = block_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.08)
           loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
           base_step = max(0.01, base_step*0.5)
           stall=0
           continue
       avg = 1.0/(2.0*n - 1.0)
       t = T
       if b.size < t: t=b.size
       part = np.argpartition(b, -t)[-t:]
       rb = np.maximum(b[part], 1e-300)
       ratio = rb / avg
       if score > 1.9:
           w = np.ones(t)
       elif score > 1.7:
           w = np.power(ratio, 1.0)
       elif score > 1.52:
           w = np.power(ratio, 2.0)
       else:
           w = np.power(ratio, 3.0)
       wsum = w.sum()
       if wsum <=0: break
       w = w / wsum
       G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
       for jj in range(t):
           k = int(part[jj])
           wi = float(w[jj])
           if wi == 0.0: continue
           i0 = k - n + 1
           if i0 < 0: i0 = 0
           i1 = k
           if i1 >= n: i1 = n - 1
           if i1 < i0: continue
           upper = k - i0
           lower = k - i1
           if lower <= 0:
               seg = a[upper::-1]
           else:
               seg = a[upper:lower-1:-1]
           G[i0:i1+1] += wi * seg
       gmean = float(G.mean())
       if not np.isfinite(gmean) or gmean <= 1e-18:
           a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
           loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
           continue
       Gn = G / gmean
       # Optional: if Gn almost constant, add small noise to break symmetry
       gvar = float(np.var(Gn))
       if gvar < 1e-12:
           noise = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
           noise_scale = float(np.abs(noise).mean())
           if noise_scale>0: Gn = Gn + 0.1 * noise/noise_scale
       steps = (base_step*0.5, base_step, base_step*2.0)
       accepted = None
       for st in steps:
           log_t = loga - st*Gn
           log_norm, a_t = normalize_log(log_t)
           score_t = score_fft(a_t)
           if score_t < best_score - 1e-12:
              accepted = (st, log_norm, a_t, score_t)
       if accepted is not None:
           st, log_norm, a_t, score_t = accepted
           a = a_t; loga = log_norm; best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score_t
           # update base_step
           if st <= base_step*0.6:
               base_step = max(0.005, base_step*0.7)
           else:
               base_step = min(0.15, max(0.005, st))
           stall=0
       else:
           # Try a random polish step if small step failed
           a_t = block_perturb(a, rng, min(0.15, base_step*2.0 + 0.02))
           score_t = score_fft(a_t)
           if score_t < best_score - 1e-12:
               a = a_t; loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY)); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_t; stall=0
           else:
               base_step *= 0.65
               if base_step < 1e-5:
                   break
       iter_count += 1
    # final random polish if time remains? We do separately.
    return best_a, best_score
```
But note: At loop start, `a` is always best, because we only update a when accepted. Random perturb not accepted unless improves; if not, a unchanged. So `score` equals best_score maybe. If we do random perturb and not accept, continue; `b` recomputed same. OK.

However, when stall>80, we set a to block_perturb(best) and continue without checking if it improves. This makes current a worse than best. Next loop score > best_score; we don't update best, but then gradient from worse a might find a path to improve, but line search accepts only if better than best. This is okay, but `a` worse and loga worse; if no improve, we may keep bad a? At next stall, we will reset to best again. But if worse a, score > best, line search may not improve best but could improve current? We only compare to best, not current. It might accept a value between current and best? If current worse, a small improvement still worse than best, not accepted; so it won't learn. Better to not accept worse perturb as current; use current=best always. Accept trial if score_t < current_score (or with probability) not necessarily best. But keep best. This allows exploration. However line search based on current score. Might be more complex.    cand_score = score_fft(candidate)
   delta = cand_score - cur_score
   if delta < 0 or rng.random() < np.exp(-delta / T_anneal): accept current=candidate; if better best update.

Let's implement `random_polish` top-level:
```
def random_polish(a_best, rng, time_limit, label):
    a_best = normalize copy
    best_a=a_best.copy(); best_score=score_fft(best_a)
    cur_a=a_best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    sigma=0.05
    T=0.02
    start=...; end=...
    it=0
    last=now
    while now<end:
       cand = block_perturb(cur_a, rng, sigma)
       sc = score_fft(cand)
       delta=sc-cur_score
       if delta < -1e-12 or (np.isfinite(delta) and rng.random() < np.exp(-delta/T)):
           cur_a=cand; cur_score=sc
           if sc < best_score:
               best_a=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
           if delta < 0:
               sigma = min(0.2, sigma*1.05)
           else:
               sigma = min(0.3, sigma*1.2)
       else:
           sigma = max(0.01, sigma*0.9)
       if delta not finite: reset cur=best
       every 500: T*=0.7
       print
    return best_a,best_score
```
But `np.exp(-delta/T)` if delta large positive overflow? delta maybe small. Use if delta/T < 700 else 0. For T small, exp underflows okay (0). Need avoid warnings: `if delta <= 0: ... else: if delta/T < 700: if rng.random() < np.exp(-delta/T): accept`.

Block perturb function:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    c=a.copy()
    # one or two blocks
    for _ in range(2):
       width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n//200), max(2, n//8)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width)))
       c[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    if rng.random() < 0.3:
       c *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma*0.5)))  # global scale? but sum normalize
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    # add tiny floor to revive zeros
    c += 1e-12
    s = c.sum()
    if s <=0: c=np.ones(n)/n
    else: c /= s
    return c
```
Global scale after normalization irrelevant (sum invariant) unless non-uniform? Multiplying all by constant cancels after normalize, so no effect. Remove global. Add floor 1e-12; sum ~1, negligible.

In random polish, block perturb may rarely improve. But with annealing can traverse. Time maybe.

Let's write `normalize_log` stable:
```
def normalize_log(loga):
    n = loga.size
    m = float(np.max(loga))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
       a = np.full(n, 1.0/n)
       return np.full(n, -np.log(n)), a
    z = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
       a = np.full(n, 1.0/n)
       return np.full(n, -np.log(n)), a
    shift = m + float(np.log(s))
    loga_norm = loga - shift
    # To avoid log of zero for next, clip loga_norm below -700? 
    loga_clip = np.clip(loga_norm, -700.0, 700.0)
    a = np.exp(loga_clip)
    asum = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(asum) or asum <= 0.0:
       a = np.full(n, 1.0/n)
       loga_clip = np.full(n, -np.log(n))
    else:
       a = a / asum
       # recompute log with tiny floor
       a_floor = np.maximum(a, TINY)
       loga_clip = np.log(a_floor)
    return loga_clip, a
```
Clipping log to -700 may add mass 1e-304*n negligible. Clipping 700 unnecessary. Use no max clip. Underflow to zero for loga_norm<-745. If many underflow, sum from others. If all underflow? But z max=1, at least one exp 0?     pool=[]
    x = np.arange(n)/ (n-1) if n>1 else [0]
    for lam in (...): add(make_exponential(x,lam)); add(make_exponential(x,-lam))

def resize_sequence(arr, n):
    n_old=arr.size
    if n_old == n: return arr.copy()
    x_old=(np.arange(n_old)+0.5)/n_old
    x_new=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    a=np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s else uniform
    return a
```

Let's refine `random_polish` sigma/T. Score differences near optimum maybe 1e-4. Use T start 0.01, min 1e-5. If delta > T*50, reject. Good.
```
def random_polish(a_best, rng, time_limit, label):
    a_best normalize
    best_a=a_best.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a_best)
    cur_a=a_best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    sigma=0.05; T=0.01
    start=time.monotonic(); end=start+time_limit
    it=0; last=now
    best_count=0
    while now<end:
       cand=block_perturb(cur_a,rng,sigma)
       sc=score_fft(cand)
       if not finite: cur_a=best_a; cur_score=best_score; continue
       delta=sc-cur_score
       accept=False
       if delta < -1e-12: accept=True
       elif delta < 0: accept=True
       else:
          if delta < 100*T: # to avoid overflow
             if rng.random() < np.exp(-delta/T): accept=True
       if accept:
          cur_a=cand; cur_score=sc
          if sc < best_score:
             best_a=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
          if delta < 0: sigma=min(0.2, sigma*1.03)
          else: sigma=min(0.3, sigma*1.1)
       else:
          sigma=max(0.01, sigma*0.95)
       # periodically cool
       if it % 200 ==0:
          T *= 0.7
          if T < 1e-6: T=1e-6
       # If stuck too long, reset current to best and increase sigma
       if it % 500 ==0 and (best_score - cur_score)>? maybe reset
       it+=1
    return best_a,best_score
```
But `np.exp(-delta/T)` if T small and delta positive but small; if T 1e-6, delta/T huge. Check `delta < 50*T` use exp. Actually if delta large, exp underflows; no need but okay with guard. Use `if delta < 700*T`.

Annealing accepting worse allows escape. Since score objective lower bound ~1, T 0.01 allows accepting 1% increases. Good.

Block perturb sigma max 0.3 might make large changes. If accepted worse with big sigma, can traverse. But could spend time far from best; keep best. Good.

Constants:
```
import numpy as np
import time
TINY = 1e-300
FLOOR = 1e-12
```

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    n=1
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n
```

`convolve_fft`:
```
def convolve_fft(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    if b.size > L:
        b = b[:L]
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)
    return b
```
`np.maximum` with out modifies b; b from ifft writable. Good.

`score_fft`:
```
def score_fft(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    s = float(a.sum())
    if n == 0 or s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s): return float('inf')
    b = convolve_fft(a)
    m = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m): return inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s*s))
```
If a sum1, can use. Good.

`block_perturb`:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    c=np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    nblocks = 1 if rng.random()<0.5 else 2
    for _ in range(nblocks):
       max_width = max(2, n//8)
       min_width = max(1, min(max_width, n//200))
       width = int(rng.integers(min_width, max_width+1))
       if width <=0: width=1
       if n > width:
           start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
       else: start=0
       c[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    c += 1e-12
    s = float(c.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
       c = np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    else:
       c = c / s
    return c
```
If n=1, width range? max_width=max(2,0)?? n//8=0 -> max_width=2, min_width=max(1,min(2,0))=1, width rng.integers(1,3) could be 1/2; if width>n, start=0; slice c[0:2] okay. Good.

def fill_initial_pool(pool, n, rng, limit):

       add_candidate(pool, np.ones(1), limit); return

`sort_pool`:
```
def sort_pool(pool):
    out=[]
    pool=list(pool)
    while pool:
       min_idx=0
       for i in range(1,len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] < pool[min_idx][0]: min_idx=i
       out.append(pool.pop(min_idx))
    return out
```
Pool small.

```
import numpy as np
import time

TINY = 1e-300

def next_pow2(x):
    n=1
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n

def normalize_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
       return np.ones(1)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
    a=a/s
    return a
```

`convolve_fft`: if n==0? not.

`score_fft`: if a.size==0 return inf.

`normalize_log`:
```
def normalize_log(loga):
    loga=np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64)
    n=loga.size
    if n==0: return loga, np.ones(0)
    m=float(np.max(loga))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
       a=np.full(n,1.0/n); return np.full(n,-np.log(n)), a
    z=np.exp(loga-m)
    s=float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       a=np.full(n,1.0/n); return np.full(n,-np.log(n)), a
    shift=m + float(np.log(s))
    log_norm=loga-shift
    a=np.exp(log_norm)
    asum=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(asum) or asum <= 0:
       a=np.full(n,1.0/n); log_norm=np.full(n,-np.log(n))
    else:
       a=a/asum
       # Add a very tiny floor only to logs, and normalize a if needed? keep a
    log_out=np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
    return log_out, a
```
If a has underflow zeros, log_out TINY. a sum1. Good.

`block_perturb`: ensure sigma positive.
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a
    c=a.copy()
    sigma=max(1e-4,float(sigma))
    num_blocks = 1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else 2
    for _ in range(num_blocks):
       min_w = max(1, n//200)
       max_w = max(min_w+1, n//8)
       if max_w >= n:
           width = max(1, int(rng.integers(1, n+1)))
       else:
           width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w+1))
       width = min(max(1,width), n)
       if n > width:
           start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
       else:
           start=0
       factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
       c[start:start+width] *= factor
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    c += 1e-12
    s=float(c.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       c=np.full(n,1.0/n)
    else: c/=s
    return c
```
If n=1, min_w=max(1,0)=1; max_w=max(2,0)=2; max_w>=n True; width rng.integers(1,2) ->1. good.

`gradient_search` as big function. Use `tmp` prealloc. Need inside use `time` and `print`.
Potential issue: In gradient_search, we use `score_fft(a_t)` many times. If score_fft returns inf due sum? no.

```
def gradient_search(a, rng, time_limit, label):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fft(a)
    time_limit = max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    if time_limit <= 0:
        return best_a, best_score
    start = time.monotonic(); end = start + time_limit
    base_step = 0.05
    T = int(min(512, max(64, n // 32)))
    tmp = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = start
    while time.monotonic() < end:
       b = convolve_fft(a)
       maxb = float(b.max())
       s = float(a.sum())
       score = 2.0 * n * maxb / (s*s)
       if not np.isfinite(score):
           a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
           loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
           continue
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score; stall=0
       else:
           stall += 1
       if best_score <= 1.42:
           break
       if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
           print(f"grad {label} it={it} cur={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} step={base_step:.4f}")
           last_print = time.monotonic()
       if stall > 60:
           a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.08)
           loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
           base_step = max(0.01, base_step*0.5)
           stall = 0
           continue
       avg = 1.0/(2.0*n - 1.0)
       t = min(T, b.size)
       if t <= 0: break
       part = np.argpartition(b, -t)[-t:]
       rb = np.maximum(b[part], 1e-300)
       ratio = rb / avg
       if score > 1.9:
           w = np.ones(t, dtype=np.float64)
       elif score > 1.7:
           w = ratio.copy()
       elif score > 1.52:
           w = ratio * ratio
       else:
           w = ratio * ratio * ratio
       wsum = float(w.sum())
       if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0:
           break
       w = w / wsum
       G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
       for jj in range(t):
           k = int(part[jj])
           wi = float(w[jj])
           if wi <= 0.0: continue
           i0 = k - n + 1
           if i0 < 0: i0 = 0
           i1 = k
           if i1 >= n: i1 = n - 1
           if i1 < i0: continue
           seg_len = i1 - i0 + 1
           upper = k - i0
           lower = k - i1
           if lower <= 0:
               seg = a[upper::-1]
           else:
               seg = a[upper:lower-1:-1]
           if seg.size != seg_len:
               G[i0:i1+1] += wi * seg
           else:
               np.multiply(seg, wi, out=tmp[:seg_len])
               G[i0:i1+1] += tmp[:seg_len]
       gmean = float(G.mean())
       if not np.isfinite(gmean) or gmean <= 1e-18:
           a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
           loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
           continue
       Gn = G / gmean
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(Gn)):
           a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
           loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,TINY))
           continue
       gmax = float(np.max(Gn))
       if gmax > 100.0:
           Gn = np.clip(Gn, 0.0, 100.0)
           gm = float(Gn.mean())
           if gm > 0: Gn = Gn / gm
       gstd = float(np.std(Gn))
       if gstd < 1e-10:
           noise = rng.random(n)
           ns = float(noise.mean())
           if ns > 0:
               Gn = Gn + 0.1 * (noise / ns)
       steps = (base_step*0.5, base_step, base_step*2.0)
       accepted = None
       for st in steps:
           log_t = loga - st * Gn
           log_norm, a_t = normalize_log(log_t)
           sc_t = score_fft(a_t)
           if np.isfinite(sc_t) and sc_t < best_score - 1e-12:
               accepted = (st, log_norm, a_t, sc_t)
       if accepted is not None:
           st, log_norm, a_t, sc_t = accepted
           a = a_t; loga = log_norm; best_a = a.copy(); best_score = sc_t; stall=0
           if st <= base_step*0.6:
               base_step = max(0.005, base_step*0.7)
           else:
               base_step = min(0.12, max(0.005, st))
       else:
           a_t = block_perturb(a, rng, min(0.15, base_step*2.0 + 0.02))
           sc_t = score_fft(a_t)
           if np.isfinite(sc_t) and sc_t < best_score - 1e-12:
               a = a_t; loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY)); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=sc_t; stall=0
           else:
               base_step *= 0.65
               if base_step < 1e-5:
                   break
       it += 1
    return best_a, best_score
```

Potential issue: `score_fft(a_t)` computes score with `s=a_t.sum()`. If `a_t` sum slightly not1 due underflow, okay. But `score_fft` may be slow and uses exact sum; We already normalize to sum1 in normalize_log (a). Good.

In `random_polish`, use best_score from score_fft. If score_fft uses sum, good.

```
def random_polish(a_best, rng, time_limit, label):
    a_best=normalize_sequence(a_best)
    best_a=a_best.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a_best)
    cur_a=a_best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    time_limit=max(0,float(time_limit))
    if time_limit<=0: return best_a,best_score
    start=time.monotonic(); end=start+time_limit
    sigma=0.05; T=0.01; it=0; last_print=start
    while time.monotonic() < end:
       cand = block_perturb(cur_a, rng, sigma)
       sc = score_fft(cand)
       if not np.isfinite(sc):
           cur_a=best_a.copy(); cur_score=best_score; continue
       delta = sc - cur_score
       accept = False
       if delta < -1e-12:
           accept = True
       else:
           if delta < 50.0 * T:
               if rng.random() < np.exp(-delta/T):
                   accept = True
       if accept:
           cur_a=cand; cur_score=sc
           if sc < best_score:
               best_a=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
           if delta < 0:
               sigma = min(0.25, sigma*1.03)
           else:
               sigma = min(0.3, sigma*1.08)
       else:
           sigma = max(0.02, sigma*0.97)
       it += 1
       if it % 300 == 0:
           T *= 0.7
           if T < 1e-6: T=1e-6
       if it % 1000 == 0 and cur_score > best_score + 0.02:
           cur_a=best_a.copy(); cur_score=best_score; sigma=min(0.2, sigma*1.5)
       if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
           print(f"polish {label} it={it} cur={cur_score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}")
           last_print=time.monotonic()
    return best_a,best_score
```
If delta positive but `delta < 50*T`; if T small, reject quickly. Good.

`make_*`:
```
def make_exponential(x, lam):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    a=np.exp(lam*(x-0.5))
    a=np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s=a.sum(); return a/s
```
Could use `normalize_sequence` but that maxes and sum. Use direct.

`make_logistic`:
```
z=lam*(x-0.5); a=1/(1+np.exp(-z)); ...
```
If z large positive, exp(-z) small; if z large negative, exp(-z) large? lam up to8, z ±4, fine. For lam large not. Use stable? not necessary. Could clip z [-30,30].
```
z=np.clip(z,-30,30)
```

`make_mixture_exp`:
```
p1=np.exp(lam1*(x-0.5)); p2=np.exp(lam2*(x-0.5)); a=w*p1+(1-w)*p2+1e-12; normalize
```
Clip lam values to ±10.

`make_power`:
```
xc=np.clip(x,1e-12,1.0); a=np.power(xc, alpha); a+=1e-12; normalize
```
For reverse, x reversed.

`make_beta`:
```
xc=np.clip(x,1e-9,1-1e-9); a=np.power(xc,alpha)*np.power(1-xc,beta); normalize
```

`make_gaussian`:
```
a=np.exp(-((x-mu)**2)/(2*sigma*sigma)); normalize
```
If sigma=0? use min 0.05.

`make_sin`:
```
a=1 + A*np.cos(2*np.pi*x + phi); a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); normalize
```

`fill_initial_pool(pool,n,rng,limit)`:
If limit <=0 return. Use `add_candidate`.
Potential duplicate candidate `lam=0` reverse same; fine.
Code:
```
def fill_initial_pool(pool, n, rng, limit):
    if n <=1:
       add_candidate(pool, np.ones(n), limit); return
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n-1)
    x05 = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    xr = x05[::-1]
    lams = [0.0,0.5,0.8,1.0,1.2,1.5,1.8,2.0,2.2,2.5,3.0,3.5,4.0,5.0]
    for lam in lams:
       add_candidate(pool, make_exponential(x05, lam), limit)
       if lam != 0.0:
          add_candidate(pool, make_exponential(x05, -lam), limit)
    for lam in [1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,4.0,5.0,7.0,9.0]:
       add_candidate(pool, make_logistic(x05, lam), limit)
       add_candidate(pool, make_logistic(x05, -lam), limit)
    for l1 in [1.0,2.0,3.0,4.0]:
       for l2 in [-4.0,-2.0,0.0]:
          for w in [0.2,0.5,0.8]:
             add_candidate(pool, make_mixture_exp(x05, l1, l2, w), limit)
    alphas = [0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,5.0,8.0]
    for alpha in alphas:
       add_candidate(pool, make_power(x05, alpha), limit)
       add_candidate(pool, make_power(xr, alpha), limit)
    betas = [(0.5,0.5),(0.2,1.0),(0.5,2.0),(0.2,5.0),(1.0,0.2),(2.0,0.5),(5.0,0.2),(1.0,2.0),(2.0,1.0),(0.2,0.2),(2.0,2.0)]
    for aa,bb in betas:
       add_candidate(pool, make_beta(x05, aa, bb), limit)
    for mu in [0.25,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.75]:
       for sig in [0.15,0.25,0.4,0.6]:
          add_candidate(pool, make_gaussian(x05, mu, sig), limit)
    for A in [0.3,0.6,0.9,1.0]:
       for phi in [0.0, np.pi/4, np.pi/2, 3*np.pi/4, np.pi, 5*np.pi/4, 3*np.pi/2, 7*np.pi/4]:
          add_candidate(pool, make_sin(x05, A, phi), limit)
    nrand = max(8, int(limit))
    for _ in range(nrand):
       a = rng.random(n) + 1e-8
       add_candidate(pool, a, limit)
       a = rng.exponential(1.0, n) + 1e-8
       add_candidate(pool, a, limit)
```
This may be many candidates (exponential 27, logistic16, mix36, power16, beta11, gaussian20, sin32, random16*? nrand=10 ->20, total178). Good.

`add_candidate` must handle arrays views and copy before computing? It normalizes and copies if keep.
```
def add_candidate(pool, arr, limit):
    if limit <=0: return
    a = normalize_sequence(arr)
    sc = score_fft(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return
    a = a.copy()
    if len(pool) < limit:
       pool.append((sc,a))
    else:
       max_idx=0
       for i in range(1, len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] > pool[max_idx][0]: max_idx=i
       if sc < pool[max_idx][0]:
          pool[max_idx]=(sc,a)
```
If limit 0, no.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """..."""
    budget_s = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    start_all = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_all + budget_s - 15.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if budget_s > 1500:
       target_n = 24576
    elif budget_s > 400:
       target_n = 16384
    else:
       target_n = 4096
    # fallback
    x = (np.arange(target_n)+0.5)/float(target_n)
    best_a = normalize_sequence(make_exponential(x, 2.0))
    best_score = safe_exact_score(best_a)
    exact_ok = np.isfinite(best_score)
    if not exact_ok:
       best_score = score_fft(best_a)
    print("fallback score", best_score)
    # height seq
    try:
       hs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
       if hs.ndim == 1 and hs.size > 0:
          hs = normalize_sequence(hs)
          # ensure sum not <0.01? sum=1; valid
          ehs = safe_exact_score(hs)
          if np.isfinite(ehs) and ehs < best_score:
              best_score = float(ehs); best_a = hs.copy()
    except Exception:
       pass
```

Target loop:
```
    max_cands = 6
    for idx in range(min(max_cands, len(target_inits))):
       now = time.monotonic()
       rem = deadline - now - 30.0
       if rem < 30: break
       if exact_ok and best_score < 1.48:
           print('good best found; stop early')
           break
       from_pool_sc, from_pool_a = target_inits[idx]
       if idx == 0:
          alloc = min(800.0, max(300.0, rem*0.45))
       elif idx == 1:
          alloc = min(500.0, max(200.0, rem*0.28))
       elif idx == 2:
          alloc = min(350.0, max(120.0, rem*0.16))
       else:
          alloc = min(220.0, max(60.0, rem*0.10))
       if alloc < 20: alloc = min(rem*0.4, 30.0)
       if alloc < 10: break
       grad_time = alloc * 0.65
       pol_time = alloc * 0.35
       a_grad, sc_grad = gradient_search(from_pool_a, rng, grad_time, 'target%d' % idx)
       now2 = time.monotonic()
       a_cand, sc_cand = a_grad, sc_grad
       if now2 < deadline - 40.0 and pol_time > 5.0:
           remaining_pol = min(pol_time, deadline - now2 - 40.0)
           if remaining_pol > 5.0:
               a_pol, sc_pol = random_polish(a_grad, rng, remaining_pol, 'polish%d' % idx)
               if sc_pol < sc_grad:
                   a_cand, sc_cand = a_pol, sc_pol
       e = safe_exact_score(a_cand)
       if np.isfinite(e):
           exact_ok = True
           if e < best_score:
               best_score = float(e); best_a = a_cand.copy()
               print('exact improved to', best_score)
       else:
           if sc_cand < best_score:
               best_score = float(sc_cand); best_a = a_cand.copy()
       print('after candidate', idx, 'best', best_score, 'time used', time.monotonic()-start_all)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

TINY = 1e-300


"""
Multi-start search for nonnegative sequences minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))**2 .

The search uses fast FFT convolution for scoring, starts from many
parametric nonnegative shapes plus a short coarse-resolution optimization,
then applies a focused steepest-descent-like update on the largest
convolution coefficients and a simulated-annealing block perturbation.
"""


def next_pow2(x):
    n = 1
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= TINY:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def convolve_fft(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    if b.size > L:
        b = b[:L]
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)
    return b


def score_fft(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    b = convolve_fft(a)
    m = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def safe_exact_score(a):
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in a]
        v = evaluate_sequence(vals)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if np.isfinite(v):
        return float(v)
    return float("inf")


def normalize_log(loga):
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64)
    n = loga.size
    if n == 0:
        return loga, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    m = float(np.max(loga))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
        return np.full(n, -np.log(float(n)), dtype=np.float64), a

    z = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
        return np.full(n, -np.log(float(n)), dtype=np.float64), a

    shift = m + float(np.log(s))
    log_norm = loga - shift
    a = np.exp(log_norm)
    asum = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(asum) or asum <= 0.0:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
        log_norm = np.full(n, -np.log(float(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a / asum

    return np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY)), a


def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a.copy()

    c = a.copy()
    sigma = max(1e-4, float(sigma))
    nblocks = 1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else 2

    for _ in range(nblocks):
        min_w = max(1, n // 200)
        max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 8)
        if max_w >= n:
            width = max(1, int(rng.integers(1, n + 1)))
        else:
            width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w + 1))
        width = min(max(1, width), n)

        if n > width:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width))
        else:
            start = 0

        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
        c[start:start + width] *= factor

    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    c += 1e-12
    s = float(c.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        c = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        c = c / s
    return c


def add_candidate(pool, arr, limit):
    if limit <= 0:
        return
    a = normalize_sequence(arr)
    sc = score_fft(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    a = a.copy()
    if len(pool) < limit:
        pool.append((sc, a))
    else:
        worst = 0
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > pool[worst][0]:
                worst = i
        if sc < pool[worst][0]:
            pool[worst] = (sc, a)


def sort_pool(pool):
    pool = list(pool)
    out = []
    while pool:
        mi = 0
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] < pool[mi][0]:
                mi = i
        out.append(pool.pop(mi))
    return out


def resize_sequence(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return normalize_sequence(a)

    x_old = (np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(m)
    x_new = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(b)


def make_exponential(x, lam):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    z = float(lam) * (x - 0.5)
    a = np.exp(z)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_logistic(x, lam):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.clip(float(lam) * (x - 0.5), -28.0, 28.0)
    a = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-z))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_mixture_exp(x, lam1, lam2, w):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    y = x - 0.5
    p1 = np.exp(float(lam1) * y)
    p2 = np.exp(float(lam2) * y)
    a = float(w) * p1 + (1.0 - float(w)) * p2
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_power(x, alpha):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    xc = np.clip(x, 1e-12, 1.0)
    a = np.power(xc, float(alpha))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_beta(x, aa, bb):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    xc = np.clip(x, 1e-9, 1.0 - 1e-9)
    a = np.power(xc, float(aa)) * np.power(1.0 - xc, float(bb))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_gaussian(x, mu, sigma):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    sigma = max(0.05, float(sigma))
    a = np.exp(-((x - float(mu)) ** 2) / (2.0 * sigma * sigma))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def make_sin(x, amp, phase):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    a = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + float(phase))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        return np.full(x.size, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return a / s


def fill_initial_pool(pool, n, rng, limit):
    if limit <= 0:
        return
    if n <= 1:
        add_candidate(pool, np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64), limit)
        return

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    x05 = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    xr = x05[::-1]

    lams = [0.0, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.2, 1.5, 1.8, 2.0, 2.2, 2.5, 3.0, 3.5, 4.0, 5.0]
    for lam in lams:
        add_candidate(pool, make_exponential(x05, lam), limit)
        if lam != 0.0:
            add_candidate(pool, make_exponential(x05, -lam), limit)

    for lam in [1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 5.0, 7.0, 9.0]:
        add_candidate(pool, make_logistic(x05, lam), limit)
        add_candidate(pool, make_logistic(x05, -lam), limit)

    for l1 in [1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0]:
        for l2 in [-4.0, -2.0, 0.0]:
            for w in [0.2, 0.5, 0.8]:
                add_candidate(pool, make_mixture_exp(x05, l1, l2, w), limit)

    alphas = [0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 8.0]
    for alpha in alphas:
        add_candidate(pool, make_power(x05, alpha), limit)
        add_candidate(pool, make_power(xr, alpha), limit)

    betas = [
        (0.5, 0.5),
        (0.2, 1.0),
        (0.5, 2.0),
        (0.2, 5.0),
        (1.0, 0.2),
        (2.0, 0.5),
        (5.0, 0.2),
        (1.0, 2.0),
        (2.0, 1.0),
        (0.2, 0.2),
        (2.0, 2.0),
    ]
    for aa, bb in betas:
        add_candidate(pool, make_beta(x05, aa, bb), limit)

    for mu in [0.25, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.75]:
        for sig in [0.15, 0.25, 0.4, 0.6]:
            add_candidate(pool, make_gaussian(x05, mu, sig), limit)

    for A in [0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.0]:
        for phi in [0.0, np.pi / 4, np.pi / 2, 3.0 * np.pi / 4,
                    np.pi, 5.0 * np.pi / 4, 3.0 * np.pi / 2, 7.0 * np.pi / 4]:
            add_candidate(pool, make_sin(x05, A, phi), limit)

    nrand = max(8, int(limit))
    for _ in range(nrand):
        a = rng.random(n) + 1e-8
        add_candidate(pool, a, limit)
        a = rng.exponential(1.0, n) + 1e-8
        add_candidate(pool, a, limit)


def gradient_search(a, rng, time_limit, label):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fft(a)

    time_limit = max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    if time_limit <= 0.0:
        return best_a, best_score

    start = time.monotonic()
    end = start + time_limit
    base_step = 0.05
    T = int(min(512, max(64, n // 32)))
    tmp = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)

    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = start

    while time.monotonic() < end:
        b = convolve_fft(a)
        maxb = float(b.max())
        s = float(a.sum())
        score = 2.0 * n * maxb / (s * s)

        if not np.isfinite(score):
            a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
            continue

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_score = score
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1

        if best_score <= 1.42:
            break

        if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
            print(f"grad {label} it={it} cur={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} step={base_step:.4f}")
            last_print = time.monotonic()

        if stall > 60:
            a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.08)
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
            base_step = max(0.01, base_step * 0.5)
            stall = 0
            continue

        avg = 1.0 / (2.0 * n - 1.0)
        t = min(T, b.size)
        if t <= 0:
            break

        part = np.argpartition(b, -t)[-t:]
        rb = np.maximum(b[part], 1e-300)
        ratio = rb / avg

        if score > 1.9:
            w = np.ones(t, dtype=np.float64)
        elif score > 1.7:
            w = ratio.copy()
        elif score > 1.52:
            w = ratio * ratio
        else:
            w = ratio * ratio * ratio

        wsum = float(w.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0.0:
            break
        w = w / wsum

        G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for jj in range(t):
            k = int(part[jj])
            wi = float(w[jj])
            if wi <= 0.0:
                continue

            i0 = k - n + 1
            if i0 < 0:
                i0 = 0
            i1 = k
            if i1 >= n:
                i1 = n - 1
            if i1 < i0:
                continue

            seg_len = i1 - i0 + 1
            upper = k - i0
            lower = k - i1

            if lower <= 0:
                seg = a[upper::-1]
            else:
                seg = a[upper:lower - 1:-1]

            if seg.size != seg_len:
                G[i0:i1 + 1] += wi * seg
            else:
                np.multiply(seg, wi, out=tmp[:seg_len])
                G[i0:i1 + 1] += tmp[:seg_len]

        gmean = float(G.mean())
        if not np.isfinite(gmean) or gmean <= 1e-18:
            a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
            continue

        Gn = G / gmean
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(Gn)):
            a = block_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
            loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
            continue

        if float(np.max(Gn)) > 100.0:
            Gn = np.clip(Gn, 0.0, 100.0)
            gm = float(Gn.mean())
            if gm > 0.0:
                Gn = Gn / gm

        if float(np.std(Gn)) < 1e-10:
            noise = rng.random(n)
            ns = float(noise.mean())
            if ns > 0.0:
                Gn = Gn + 0.1 * (noise / ns)

        steps = (base_step * 0.5, base_step, base_step * 2.0)
        accepted = None

        for st in steps:
            log_t = loga - st * Gn
            log_norm, a_t = normalize_log(log_t)
            sc_t = score_fft(a_t)
            if np.isfinite(sc_t) and sc_t < best_score - 1e-12:
                accepted = (st, log_norm, a_t, sc_t)

        if accepted is not None:
            st, log_norm, a_t, sc_t = accepted
            a = a_t
            loga = log_norm
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_score = sc_t
            stall = 0

            if st <= base_step * 0.6:
                base_step = max(0.005, base_step * 0.7)
            else:
                base_step = min(0.12, max(0.005, st))
        else:
            a_t = block_perturb(a, rng, min(0.15, base_step * 2.0 + 0.02))
            sc_t = score_fft(a_t)
            if np.isfinite(sc_t) and sc_t < best_score - 1e-12:
                a = a_t
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, TINY))
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_score = sc_t
                stall = 0
            else:
                base_step *= 0.65
                if base_step < 1e-5:
                    break

        it += 1

    return best_a, best_score


def random_polish(a_best, rng, time_limit, label):
    a_best = normalize_sequence(a_best)
    best_a = a_best.copy()
    best_score = score_fft(a_best)

    cur_a = a_best.copy()
    cur_score = best_score

    time_limit = max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    if time_limit <= 0.0:
        return best_a, best_score

    start = time.monotonic()
    end = start + time_limit
    sigma = 0.05
    T = 0.01
    it = 0
    last_print = start

    while time.monotonic() < end:
        cand = block_perturb(cur_a, rng, sigma)
        sc = score_fft(cand)

        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            cur_a = best_a.copy()
            cur_score = best_score
            continue

        delta = sc - cur_score
        accept = False

        if delta < -1e-12:
            accept = True
        elif delta < 50.0 * T:
            if rng.random() < np.exp(-delta / T):
                accept = True

        if accept:
            cur_a = cand
            cur_score = sc
            if sc < best_score:
                best_a = cand.copy()
                best_score = sc

            if delta < 0.0:
                sigma = min(0.25, sigma * 1.03)
            else:
                sigma = min(0.30, sigma * 1.08)
        else:
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.97)

        it += 1
        if it % 300 == 0:
            T *= 0.7
            if T < 1e-6:
                T = 1e-6

        if it % 1000 == 0 and cur_score > best_score + 0.02:
            cur_a = best_a.copy()
            cur_score = best_score
            sigma = min(0.20, sigma * 1.5)

        if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
            print(f"polish {label} it={it} cur={cur_score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}")
            last_print = time.monotonic()

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Search for a nonnegative sequence with low evaluated score.

    The method is multi-start: it samples parametric nonnegative shapes,
    optimizes a coarse proxy problem, upsamples good coarse solutions,
    then refines target-length candidates using focused local search and
    annealed block perturbations.
    """
    budget_s = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    start_all = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_all + budget_s - 15.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget_s > 1500:
        target_n = 20480
    elif budget_s > 500:
        target_n = 8192
    else:
        target_n = 2048

    x = (np.arange(target_n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(target_n)
    best_a = normalize_sequence(make_exponential(x, 2.0))
    best_score = safe_exact_score(best_a)
    exact_ok = np.isfinite(best_score)
    if not exact_ok:
        best_score = score_fft(best_a)
    print(f"fallback length={target_n} score={best_score:.6f} exact_ok={exact_ok}")

    try:
        hs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if hs.ndim == 1 and hs.size > 0:
            hs = normalize_sequence(hs)
            ehs = safe_exact_score(hs)
            if np.isfinite(ehs) and ehs < best_score:
                best_score = float(ehs)
                best_a = hs.copy()
                print(f"height_sequence_1 improved to {best_score:.6f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    coarse_sorted = []
    if deadline - start_all > 600.0:
        coarse_n = 4096
        coarse_time = min(240.0, max(60.0, 0.12 * budget_s))
        coarse_pool = []
        fill_initial_pool(coarse_pool, coarse_n, rng, 5)
        coarse_inits = sort_pool(coarse_pool)

        if coarse_inits:
            coarse_results = []
            t0 = time.monotonic()
            used = 0.0
            cnt = min(4, len(coarse_inits))
            for idx in range(cnt):
                rem = coarse_time - used - 3.0
                if rem < 10.0:
                    break
                alloc = min(80.0, rem / max(1, cnt - idx))
                a_opt, sc_opt = gradient_search(coarse_inits[idx][1], rng, alloc, f"coarse{idx}")
                coarse_results.append((sc_opt, a_opt))
                used = time.monotonic() - t0

            if coarse_results:
                coarse_sorted = sort_pool(coarse_results)
                print(f"coarse best fft score={coarse_sorted[0][0]:.6f}")

    target_pool = []
    fill_initial_pool(target_pool, target_n, rng, 12)

    for i in range(min(2, len(coarse_sorted))):
        a_up = resize_sequence(coarse_sorted[i][1], target_n)
        add_candidate(target_pool, a_up, 12)

    target_inits = sort_pool(target_pool)
    if target_inits:
        print(f"best target initial fft score={target_inits[0][0]:.6f} length={target_n}")

    max_cands = 6
    for idx in range(min(max_cands, len(target_inits))):
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = deadline - now - 30.0
        if rem < 30.0:
            break

        if exact_ok and best_score < 1.48:
            print("Good exact score already found; stopping early.")
            break

        init_score, init_a = target_inits[idx]

        if idx == 0:
            alloc = min(800.0, max(300.0, rem * 0.45))
        elif idx == 1:
            alloc = min(500.0, max(200.0, rem * 0.28))
        elif idx == 2:
            alloc = min(350.0, max(120.0, rem * 0.16))
        else:
            alloc = min(220.0, max(60.0, rem * 0.10))

        if alloc < 20.0:
            alloc = min(rem * 0.4, 30.0)
        if alloc < 10.0:
            break

        grad_time = alloc * 0.65
        pol_time = alloc * 0.35

        a_grad, sc_grad = gradient_search(init_a, rng, grad_time, f"target{idx}")
        a_cand, sc_cand = a_grad, sc_grad

        now2 = time.monotonic()
        remaining_pol = min(pol_time, deadline - now2 - 40.0)
        if remaining_pol > 5.0:
            a_pol, sc_pol = random_polish(a_grad, rng, remaining_pol, f"polish{idx}")
            if sc_pol < sc_grad:
                a_cand, sc_cand = a_pol, sc_pol

        e = safe_exact_score(a_cand)
        if np.isfinite(e):
            exact_ok = True
            if e < best_score:
                best_score = float(e)
                best_a = a_cand.copy()
                print(f"exact improved to {best_score:.6f} after target{idx}")
        else:
            if sc_cand < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc_cand)
                best_a = a_cand.copy()
                print(f"fft improved to {best_score:.6f} after target{idx}")

        print(f"candidate {idx}: initial={init_score:.6f} final_fft={sc_cand:.6f} overall_best={best_score:.6f}")

    best_a = normalize_sequence(best_a)
    final_e = safe_exact_score(best_a)
    if np.isfinite(final_e) and final_e < best_score:
        best_score = float(final_e)

    print(f"final length={best_a.size} reported_best={best_score:.6f}")
    return [float(v) for v in best_a]
```
